        Idea:
Sprint 2 of the megaplan cloud feature — extend core megaplan ergonomics, add local + ssh providers, ship toolchain extensibility in cloud.yaml, retire the old reigh-megaplan-dev folder.

### Context
Sprint 1 shipped in v0.19.0 on 2026-04-21: built-in megaplan cloud subcommand (init/build/deploy/status/attach/logs/exec/resume/down/destroy) with a Railway provider, extracted from the ad-hoc setup at ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/. Spec schema lives in megaplan/cloud/spec.py, templates in megaplan/cloud/templates/, providers in megaplan/cloud/providers/.

Architectural principle established in sprint 1 and now codified: CORE megaplan owns capabilities, CLOUD subcommands are thin transport wrappers. Do not reinvent chain state parsing in cloud. Do not grow a parallel 'cloud chain *' surface. Core gets the command; cloud wraps it for remote execution.

### Sprint 2 deliverables

Core megaplan additions (must work locally without any cloud dependency):
1. megaplan chain start --spec <path> — validate spec, resolve referenced idea files, kick auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the 'init + auto' pair for chain runs.
2. megaplan chain status --spec <path> — read chain_state.json, pretty-print current_milestone, completed, remaining, per-milestone status. Unit tests against synthetic chain states.
3. megaplan init --idea-file <path> with optional --auto-start flag — read idea from a file instead of the positional CLI arg.

Cloud additions (thin wrappers over the new core commands):
4. megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml> — upload spec, then cloud exec to call megaplan chain start remotely.
5. megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file> — upload idea file, then cloud exec to call megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start remotely.
6. megaplan cloud status --chain — fetch remote chain_state.json, invoke the core chain-status formatter on it. Do NOT reinvent the parser.

New providers:
7. Local provider (docker compose) — fastest iteration target, no Railway/cloud dependency. Becomes the CI-friendly smoke-test target.
8. SSH provider (plain docker-over-ssh) — 'any host with docker + ssh access' story.

Toolchain extensibility:
9. toolchains: block in cloud.yaml — declares one or more toolchains (rust, go, java, or custom with user-provided install snippet). Template renderer appends corresponding Dockerfile stages. Without this, the image is Python/Node only.

Hardening + retirement:
10. Secret redaction in cloud logs output (match known secret name patterns, replace with ***REDACTED***).
11. Clear error messages + documentation link when railway or docker CLI is missing at runtime.
12. ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ migration — deploy cloud with a cloud.yaml matching reigh's current env, run a tiny plan to verify parity, then delete the folder and replace with a MIGRATED.md pointer.

### Hard constraints
- Core commands MUST work locally without any cloud code loaded.
- Cloud wrappers MUST call out to core commands, not reimplement.
- Fly provider is OPTIONAL (skip if time-pressured).
- Preserve v0.19.0's public surface; this is additive.
- No breaking cloud.yaml changes from v0.19.0 (add fields, don't rename).
- Unit tests for every new core command; mocked-subprocess tests for every new provider; smoke test for local provider docker-compose lifecycle.

### Explicit non-goals
- No Kubernetes provider.
- No chain-of-chains.
- No cloud-specific chain status parser (the whole point of the core-first architecture).
- No version bump in this plan — sprint 2 ships as v0.20.0 but the bump + CHANGELOG commit is a separate follow-up.

### References
- Sprint 1 landed in v0.19.0 (2026-04-21). Full CHANGELOG entry in CHANGELOG.md.
- Reigh parity reference: ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ and megaplan/cloud/_reference/ (gitignored local copy of same).
- Verified launcher: ./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan (system python is 3.8, fails on NotRequired).
- Sprint 1 target retrospective: cloud subcommand CLI is already wired via megaplan/cloud/cli.py and dispatched from megaplan/cli.py.

Robustness: standard — feature-rich with architecture decisions to enforce, multiple independent pieces worth independent critique, gate useful for scope discipline.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Cloud Sprint 2 — Core Ergonomics, Local/SSH Providers, Toolchain Block, Reigh Retirement

## Overview

**Goal.** Extend megaplan's cloud story to sprint-2 parity with the brief's deliverables: three new **core** commands (`chain start`, `chain status` pretty-print, `init --idea-file`/`--auto-start`) that work with zero cloud dependencies, three **cloud** wrappers that thin-call those core commands (`cloud chain`, `cloud bootstrap`, `cloud status --chain`), two new **providers** (`local` docker-compose, `ssh` docker-over-ssh), a **toolchains** extensibility block in `cloud.yaml`, log **secret redaction**, clear missing-CLI errors, and the retirement of `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`.

**Architectural contract (from sprint 1).** Core owns capability; cloud wraps transport. The whole point of the new core commands is that `cloud chain`, `cloud bootstrap`, `cloud status --chain` must be *thin* — upload a file, shell the same core command remotely, pretty-print the structured payload locally. No re-parsing `chain_state.json` in cloud code.

**Current shape.**
- Core: `megaplan/chain.py` already has `ChainSpec`/`ChainState`/`load_chain_state`, drives via `auto.drive()`, and exposes `chain status --spec` as a JSON dumper. `megaplan/handlers.py:handle_init` takes `idea` as a positional arg only.
- Cloud: `megaplan/cloud/{cli.py,spec.py,template.py,providers/base.py,providers/railway.py}`, templates under `megaplan/cloud/templates/`, wrappers under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/`. `spec.py` explicitly rejects non-railway providers (`SPRINT_TWO_PROVIDERS = ("fly", "ssh", "local")`). Provider registry in `providers/base.py:get_provider` is a hardcoded `if name != "railway"` guard.

**Constraints that matter.**
- Core commands must import nothing from `megaplan.cloud` — verified by a test that monkeypatches `sys.modules` to block cloud imports and still runs the commands.
- `cloud.yaml` v0.19.0 spec must keep loading unchanged; toolchain/additions are strictly additive.
- Fly provider is explicitly **skipped** (brief marks it optional).
- No version bump / CHANGELOG entry in this plan — handled in the follow-up release commit.

## Phase 1: Core-first commands (no cloud dependency)

### Step 1: Add `megaplan chain start` subcommand (`megaplan/chain.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `build_chain_parser` (`megaplan/chain.py:528`) with a new `start` subparser under `chain_sub`. Flags: `--spec <path>` (required), `--no-git-refresh` (mirrors the current top-level flag). Keep the bare `megaplan chain --spec` path working so existing shell wrappers (including `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-chain`) don't break.
2. **Wire** `run_chain_cli` (`megaplan/chain.py:558`) to dispatch `chain start` to the same `run_chain(...)` driver the bare command uses. `chain start` validates the spec + idea files up front, then kicks the auto-loop per milestone — the existing `validate_paths` + `run_chain` path already does both. No new driver logic.
3. **Leave** the existing `chain status` subcommand in place; Step 2 enriches its output.
4. **Update** `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-chain` to call `megaplan chain start --spec "$1"` so the deployed container boots via the new named entry.

### Step 2: Add `chain status` pretty-print formatter (`megaplan/chain.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a `format_chain_status(spec: ChainSpec, state: ChainState) -> dict` helper that returns a structured payload with:
   - `current_milestone` — label and index, or `None` if pre-seed
   - `completed` — list of `{label, plan, status}` entries from `state.completed`
   - `remaining` — list of `{label, idea}` for milestones past `current_milestone_index`
   - `per_milestone` — one entry per milestone: `{label, status}` where status is one of `completed | in_progress | pending`
   - `seed_plan`, `current_plan_name`, `last_state`
2. **Rewrite** the `chain status` branch in `run_chain_cli` to call the formatter and emit both a pretty text block (to stderr via `writer`) and the structured JSON (to stdout). Preserve the existing keys (`success`, `spec`, `milestone_count`, `seed_plan`, `chain_state`) so any existing callers remain valid; add a `summary` key containing the structured payload. This is additive — no schema break.
3. **Export** `format_chain_status` from `megaplan.chain` so the cloud wrapper (Step 9) can reuse it verbatim.

### Step 3: `megaplan init --idea-file` with optional `--auto-start` (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Adjust** `init_parser` in `megaplan/cli.py:835-852`:
   - Make the `idea` positional optional (`nargs="?"`).
   - Add `--idea-file <path>` (mutually exclusive with positional `idea` — enforce in `handle_init`, not argparse, so the error flows through `CliError`).
   - Add `--auto-start` (boolean).
2. **Update** `handle_init` (`megaplan/handlers.py:728`):
   - Resolve `idea` text from either the positional arg or `--idea-file` (read as UTF-8 text, validate non-empty, raise `CliError("invalid_args", ...)` on conflict/missing).
   - Continue to persist the *idea text* (not the path) into `state["idea"]`, matching current behavior so downstream prompts are unaffected.
   - When `--auto-start` is set, after `save_state(...)` succeeds and the response is built, invoke `megaplan.auto.drive(plan_name, cwd=root, ...)` **in-process** with default driver knobs. Emit a combined response with the init payload plus `auto_outcome` containing `DriverOutcome.to_json()`.
3. **Import rule:** `auto` already imports core-only modules; `handlers.py` already imports from `auto` indirectly via `execution.py`, so no cloud coupling is introduced.

### Step 4: Core tests (`tests/test_chain.py`, `tests/test_handle_init_doc_mode.py` or new `tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `tests/test_chain.py` with:
   - `test_chain_start_invokes_driver` — mocks `auto.drive`, asserts `run_chain_cli(... chain_action="start", spec=...)` runs the same validator + driver path.
   - `test_format_chain_status_pretty` — feeds synthetic `ChainSpec` + `ChainState` (seed done, 1 completed milestone, 1 in-progress, 2 remaining) and asserts the `current_milestone`/`completed`/`remaining`/`per_milestone` fields.
2. **Add** `tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py` with cases:
   - `test_init_reads_idea_file` (file → state.idea)
   - `test_init_rejects_both_positional_and_idea_file`
   - `test_init_auto_start_invokes_drive` (mocked `auto.drive`, asserts called once with the new plan name and root)
3. **Add** `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py` that `importlib.reload`s `megaplan.cli` / `megaplan.chain` / `megaplan.handlers` with `sys.modules["megaplan.cloud"] = None` set to a raising sentinel, runs `megaplan chain status --spec` and `megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start` via `main(argv=...)`, asserts both succeed without ever importing `megaplan.cloud.*`.

## Phase 2: Provider plumbing + Local + SSH providers

### Step 5: Unlock `local` and `ssh` providers in the spec loader (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Change** `load_spec` (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py:167-172`): drop the sprint-1 gate. Accept `"railway" | "local" | "ssh"`. Leave `"fly"` in a `future_providers` tuple with a distinct error message so users aren't silently left with unhelpful errors. Update `SPRINT_TWO_PROVIDERS` to `FUTURE_PROVIDERS = ("fly",)`.
2. **Add** `LocalSpec` (fields: `compose_project: str = "megaplan-cloud"`, `workdir: str | None = None`) and `SshSpec` (fields: `host: str`, `user: str = "root"`, `port: int = 22`, `identity_file: str | None = None`, `remote_dir: str = "/var/lib/megaplan-cloud"`). Parse both in `load_spec` when the provider matches; otherwise keep them `None`.
3. **Update** `CloudSpec` dataclass to add `local: LocalSpec | None = None` and `ssh: SshSpec | None = None`. Strict-additive — field order unchanged, all existing `CloudSpec(...)` call sites in tests keep working.
4. **Update** existing tests in `tests/test_cloud_spec.py`: drop `test_load_spec_rejects_*_sprint_two_providers` (or rename to assert `"fly"` is still rejected). Add happy-path tests for `provider: local` and `provider: ssh` including required-field validation.

### Step 6: `Provider` base + registry updates (`megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** two optional abstract hooks on `Provider`:
   - `upload_file(self, src: Path, dest: str) -> None` — used by `cloud chain` / `cloud bootstrap` to push a spec or idea file to the remote workspace. Default implementation on the base class raises `CliError("not_implemented", ...)`; Railway + SSH + Local override.
   - `read_remote_file(self, path: str) -> str` — used by `cloud status --chain` to fetch `chain_state.json` text. Same default/override pattern.
2. **Replace** the hardcoded `if name != "railway"` block (`base.py:52-54`) with a name→class registry dict, importing providers lazily to keep startup lean. Unknown names raise `CliError("invalid_spec", f"Unknown cloud provider '{name}'. Known: railway, local, ssh")`.
3. **Implement** `upload_file` / `read_remote_file` on `RailwayProvider` using `railway ssh --service ... -- "cat > dest"` (base64 round-trip for safety) and `railway ssh -- cat <path>` respectively.

### Step 7: Local provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/local.py`, `megaplan/cloud/templates/docker-compose.yaml.tmpl`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `megaplan/cloud/templates/docker-compose.yaml.tmpl` — single `agent` service built from the materialized `Dockerfile`, bind-mounting a host workspace dir (`./workspace` inside the deploy dir), exposing `${PORT}`, env from a `.env` file the provider writes with the resolved secrets.
2. **Create** `megaplan/cloud/providers/local.py::LocalProvider` implementing the full `Provider` ABC:
   - `build` → `docker compose -p <proj> build`.
   - `deploy` → write `.env` with secrets, then `docker compose -p <proj> up -d`.
   - `ssh_exec` → `docker compose -p <proj> exec -T agent bash -lc '<command>'`.
   - `attach` → `docker compose -p <proj> exec agent tmux attach -t agent` (falls through to `bash -l` if no tmux session).
   - `logs` → `docker compose -p <proj> logs -f agent` (or without `-f` when `follow=False`).
   - `status_payload` → same remote `megaplan status` invocation as Railway, using `ssh_exec`.
   - `down` → `docker compose -p <proj> down` (keeps volume).
   - `destroy` → `docker compose -p <proj> down -v`.
   - `upload_file` → `docker compose cp <src> agent:<dest>`.
   - `read_remote_file` → `docker compose exec -T agent cat <path>`.
3. **Guard** provider construction: if `docker` is not on PATH, raise `CliError("provider_unavailable", "docker CLI not found — install: https://docs.docker.com/get-docker/")`. Mirrors the Railway pattern (`railway.py:27-29`).
4. **Materialization:** extend `materialize_deploy_dir` in `megaplan/cloud/template.py:157` to emit `docker-compose.yaml` when `spec.provider == "local"` (new helper `_render_resource_template` call already exists).

### Step 8: SSH provider (`megaplan/cloud/providers/ssh.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `megaplan/cloud/providers/ssh.py::SshProvider`:
   - Construction requires `ssh` on PATH (reuse the `shutil.which` + `CliError` pattern). Resolves identity/user/host/port from `SshSpec`.
   - `_remote_run(cmd: str)` runs `ssh [-i identity] -p port user@host -- bash -lc '<cmd>'`.
   - `build` → rsync deploy dir to `<remote_dir>`, then `_remote_run("cd <remote_dir> && docker build -t megaplan-cloud-<proj> .")`. Requires `rsync` on both sides; if missing locally, fall back to `scp -r` with an info-level warning.
   - `deploy` → write env file remotely, then `docker run -d --name <container> --env-file ... -v <vol>:/workspace -p <port>:<port> megaplan-cloud-<proj>`.
   - `ssh_exec` → `_remote_run("docker exec <container> bash -lc '<cmd>'")`.
   - `attach` → `ssh ... -t docker exec -it <container> tmux attach -t agent`.
   - `logs` → `ssh ... docker logs <-f> <container>`.
   - `status_payload`, `down`, `destroy`, `upload_file`, `read_remote_file` follow the Railway pattern over `_remote_run`.
2. **Quoting safety:** use `shlex.quote` on every user-provided string that's interpolated into the remote command line (paths, env file names, container name). This mirrors the existing pattern in `cloud/cli.py:153`.

### Step 9: Provider tests (`tests/test_cloud_local.py`, `tests/test_cloud_ssh.py`, `tests/test_cloud_local_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Unit tests (mocked subprocess)** — pattern from `tests/test_cloud_railway.py`:
   - `tests/test_cloud_local.py` monkeypatches `subprocess.run` and `shutil.which`, exercises each `LocalProvider` method, asserts exact argv for `docker compose ...` commands and that the materialized `docker-compose.yaml` references the expected service/volume/port.
   - `tests/test_cloud_ssh.py` similar for `SshProvider` — asserts each path produces the right `ssh user@host -- '<command>'` argv with `shlex.quote` applied.
2. **Local docker-compose smoke test** — `tests/test_cloud_local_lifecycle.py`, `@pytest.mark.slow`, skipped if `docker compose` isn't reachable (same skip pattern as `tests/test_cloud_docker_build.py:19-32`). Steps: materialize deploy dir for a minimal `mode: idle` spec, `compose build`, `compose up -d`, `exec agent which megaplan`, `compose down -v`. One lifecycle cycle; under 5 minutes.

### Step 10: Toolchain extensibility in `cloud.yaml` (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py`, `megaplan/cloud/template.py`, `megaplan/cloud/templates/Dockerfile`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Spec:** add `ToolchainSpec` dataclass and parse a top-level `toolchains:` list in `load_spec`. Each entry is either:
   - a string alias — `rust`, `go`, or `java` — resolved to a built-in install recipe; or
   - a mapping — `{name: <custom-label>, install: "<shell snippet>"}` where `install` is a multi-line shell script run as a `RUN` layer.
   Validate that every alias is known and every custom entry has non-empty `install`. Default: empty list (baseline Python+Node image).
2. **Template:** convert `megaplan/cloud/templates/Dockerfile` into a templated string keyed by a new `TOOLCHAIN_BLOCK` placeholder. Render the block in `template.py` by looking up built-in recipes (kept in a `_TOOLCHAIN_RECIPES` dict in `template.py`) and concatenating user snippets as additional `RUN` stages inserted after the Node install and before `COPY entrypoint.sh`. Each recipe also updates `ENV PATH` if needed (e.g. `/root/.cargo/bin` for rust, `/usr/local/go/bin` for go).
3. **Docs:** update `megaplan/cloud/templates/cloud.yaml.tmpl` with a commented `toolchains:` example showing one alias and one custom entry.
4. **Tests:** extend `tests/test_cloud_template.py`:
   - `test_toolchain_rust_renders_cargo_install`
   - `test_toolchain_custom_snippet_renders_verbatim`
   - `test_toolchain_unknown_alias_rejected`
   - `test_no_toolchain_block_leaves_dockerfile_unchanged` — preserves the "no breaking change from v0.19.0" constraint by diffing against the frozen baseline.

## Phase 3: Cloud wrappers + hardening + migration

### Step 11: `megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml>` and `cloud bootstrap <idea-file>` (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `build_cloud_parser` (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:22`) with two new subparsers:
   - `chain` — positional `spec` (path on disk), optional `--remote-dir` (default derives from `spec.repo.workspace`).
   - `bootstrap` — positional `idea_file`, required `--plan-name`, optional `--robustness` (default `"standard"`).
2. **Dispatch in `run_cloud_cli`:**
   - `cloud chain`: resolve the spec file locally, `provider.upload_file(local_path, f"{workspace}/chain.yaml")`, then `provider.ssh_exec(f"cd {workspace} && megaplan chain start --spec {remote_path}")`. Relay output, surface exit code.
   - `cloud bootstrap`: upload idea file to `{workspace}/idea.txt`, then `provider.ssh_exec(f"cd {workspace} && megaplan init --project-dir {workspace} --name {plan} --idea-file {idea_path} --auto-start --robustness {robustness}")`. Relay output.
   - Both commands **must** call existing core subcommands — no cloud-side parsing of chain state or plan state.

### Step 12: `megaplan cloud status --chain` (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `--chain` flag to the existing `status` subparser.
2. **Implementation in `run_cloud_cli`:** when `--chain` is set, resolve the cloud spec's `chain.spec` (remote path) → compute the remote `chain_state.json` path with the same `_state_path_for` logic that `chain.py:205` uses. Fetch via `provider.read_remote_file(state_path)`, parse with `ChainState.from_dict(...)`, also fetch the chain spec YAML and parse with `chain.load_spec(...)`, then call `chain.format_chain_status(spec, state)` (imported from Step 2). Emit the same structured+pretty payload the local `chain status` prints.
3. **Contract test** (`tests/test_cloud_chain_status.py`): mock `provider.read_remote_file` to return canned YAML + JSON; assert `cloud status --chain` prints a payload structurally identical to what local `chain status --spec` produces for the same inputs. This is the guardrail that enforces "no cloud-specific chain status parser."

### Step 13: Secret redaction in cloud logs/exec output (`megaplan/cloud/redact.py`, provider call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `megaplan/cloud/redact.py` with a single `redact(text: str, secret_names: list[str], env: Mapping[str, str] | None = None) -> str` function. Replaces:
   - Literal known secret values drawn from `env` (default `os.environ`) for every name in `secret_names` — only when value length ≥ 8 to avoid over-matching.
   - A compiled pattern matching `<NAME>=<value>` and `<NAME>: <value>` for each secret name, replacing the value with `***REDACTED***`.
   - A default regex catching anything that looks like an obvious API key (e.g. `sk-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}`, `ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}`).
2. **Wire** redaction at the point output is relayed to the user. Touch points:
   - `cloud/cli.py::_relay_output` (currently at `cli.py:227`) — redact `result.stdout` / `result.stderr` before writing.
   - Provider `logs()` (non-follow case only — follow-mode streams directly through the child, no interception). Document the follow-mode gap in the helper's docstring.
3. **Tests** (`tests/test_cloud_redact.py`): table-driven cases asserting literal values, `NAME=value` style, `NAME: value` style, and the `sk-...`/`ghp_...` default patterns all redact.

### Step 14: Missing-CLI errors with documentation links (`megaplan/cloud/providers/{railway,local,ssh}.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Standardize** the provider-unavailable messages to include both "what's missing" and "where to install":
   - Railway: already uses `INSTALL_LINK` — leave intact, but wrap in a common formatter.
   - Docker (Local + any usage inside Railway for `docker build`): `docker CLI not found. Install Docker: https://docs.docker.com/get-docker/`
   - SSH: `ssh not on PATH`; `rsync not on PATH (using scp fallback — 10× slower)` — downgraded to a warning, not an error, via `sys.stderr.write`.
2. **Ensure** all three errors come through as `CliError("provider_unavailable", ...)` so the existing `_emit_error` serializer in `cloud/cli.py:234` produces a consistent JSON shape.

### Step 15: Retire `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` (reigh-migration.md in megaplan docs/, user-run steps)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Draft** a short runbook at `docs/cloud-migration-from-reigh.md` (this is the one doc this plan adds — needed because the folder is outside the megaplan repo and the user has to run the migration themselves). Steps:
   - `megaplan cloud init --cloud-yaml ./cloud.yaml`
   - Copy the env values from the current reigh `.env` into local env vars (or a `.env` committed with care — `.gitignore` already excludes it post-v0.19.0).
   - `megaplan cloud build && megaplan cloud deploy`
   - Run `megaplan cloud bootstrap ./idea.txt --plan-name reigh-parity-smoke` for a tiny verify plan.
   - `megaplan cloud status` → expect `done`.
   - Delete `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` and replace with `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/MIGRATED.md` (single-line pointer to `megaplan cloud` and the runbook).
2. **Deliverable from this plan:** the runbook markdown + the `cloud bootstrap` flow it depends on. The actual folder deletion is a human-executed step — flagged as `info` in success criteria since the reigh folder lives outside this repo's sandbox.

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 1 first (Steps 1–4).** Core commands unlock everything downstream: Step 11 (`cloud chain`/`cloud bootstrap`) and Step 12 (`cloud status --chain`) reuse `chain.format_chain_status` and `init --idea-file` as-is. Land + tested before any cloud wrapper work.
2. **Phase 2 provider plumbing (Steps 5–6)** before the concrete providers (Steps 7–8): unlocking the spec and expanding the base class are cheap, and getting them right prevents rework on the two concrete providers.
3. **Concrete providers (Steps 7–8) + tests (Step 9)** can proceed in parallel for local and ssh — they share no code.
4. **Toolchain block (Step 10)** is independent of providers; can be landed any time after Phase 1 but needs to ship before the reigh migration so the reigh cloud.yaml can specify the same toolchains reigh currently uses.
5. **Phase 3 (Steps 11–14)** after Phases 1–2 are green.
6. **Reigh retirement (Step 15)** last — requires every earlier deliverable to be landed.

## Validation Order
1. Unit tests for every new/changed core module first (`pytest tests/test_chain.py tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py tests/test_core_without_cloud.py -x`) — cheapest and catches the core/cloud isolation contract immediately.
2. Cloud unit tests (`pytest tests/test_cloud_spec.py tests/test_cloud_local.py tests/test_cloud_ssh.py tests/test_cloud_template.py tests/test_cloud_redact.py -x`) — all mocked-subprocess, fast.
3. Full test suite (`pytest`) — ensure no regression in pre-existing cloud / chain / handler tests.
4. Local docker-compose smoke test (`pytest -m slow tests/test_cloud_local_lifecycle.py`) — run once pre-merge on a host with docker.
5. Manual end-to-end: deploy a `provider: local` spec with `mode: idle`, run `cloud bootstrap` against a trivial idea file, watch it go → done via `cloud status`. Then `cloud destroy`.
6. Reigh migration is a human-run step — use the runbook (Step 15) to verify parity (a tiny plan completes end-to-end against the new in-tree deployment with the same env as reigh's).

  "hash": "sha256:4cbfdd9455f3eee192971ffc497ca481ed5b732d5b1897b28b3a78fb4184e11a",
  "questions": [
    "Toolchain entry format \u2014 is a mixed list of either strings (built-in aliases like `rust`/`go`/`java`) OR mappings (`{name, install}` for custom) acceptable, or do you prefer a single uniform shape (e.g. always `{name, install?}`)? The uniform shape is more verbose but easier to validate.",
    "`megaplan init --auto-start` \u2014 run `auto.drive()` in-process and block the CLI (simple, matches `chain`'s model) or fire-and-forget via tmux like the cloud entrypoint does (so `cloud bootstrap` returns quickly)? I've assumed in-process/blocking; `cloud bootstrap` then streams output until terminal.",
    "Local provider shape \u2014 single-container `docker compose` with one `agent` service bind-mounted to a host workspace dir, OR a multi-container compose file that mirrors the Railway shape (agent + separate healthcheck)? I've assumed single-container; the healthserver.py already runs inside the agent image.",
    "Reigh retirement \u2014 should the plan deliverable include a script or command that actually deletes `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` and writes `MIGRATED.md`, or just a runbook that the user executes manually? The folder is outside this repo's sandbox so automated deletion requires out-of-working-dir permission; I've assumed manual.",
    "Secret redaction scope \u2014 redact only on the non-streaming paths (capture-then-redact-then-emit), or also wrap the follow-mode `logs -f` stream with a line-buffered redactor? The follow-mode redactor is more invasive (needs stream parsing); I've assumed non-streaming only, with a docstring caveat."
  ],
  "success_criteria": [
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan chain start --spec <path>` runs the existing driver end-to-end on a synthetic spec; a new unit test asserts `run_chain_cli` reaches `run_chain(...)` via the `start` action.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan chain status --spec <path>` emits a structured payload with `current_milestone`, `completed`, `remaining`, `per_milestone`, plus a human-readable block; a new unit test covers a seed+3-milestone fixture.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan init --idea-file <path>` reads idea text from disk, persists it to `state.idea`, and errors if the positional arg is also supplied.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan init --idea-file <path> --auto-start` invokes `megaplan.auto.drive` exactly once after init and returns a response whose `auto_outcome` reflects the driver's terminal status.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Core commands (`chain start`, `chain status`, `init --idea-file --auto-start`) succeed in an environment where `import megaplan.cloud` is force-failed, verified by a dedicated isolation test.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`cloud.yaml` with `provider: local` loads, and `LocalProvider` implements every abstract method on `Provider`; mocked-subprocess tests assert exact `docker compose ...` argv for build/deploy/exec/attach/logs/down/destroy.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`cloud.yaml` with `provider: ssh` loads, and `SshProvider` implements every abstract method; mocked-subprocess tests assert `ssh`-over-`docker` argv with `shlex.quote` applied to every interpolated field.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan cloud chain <spec>` uploads the spec file via `provider.upload_file` and then calls `megaplan chain start --spec <remote>` via `provider.ssh_exec`; integration test mocks both provider methods and asserts the exact remote command string.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file> --plan-name ...` uploads the idea file and then calls `megaplan init --idea-file ... --auto-start` remotely; integration test asserts the remote command contains `--auto-start` and the uploaded remote path.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`megaplan cloud status --chain` pulls remote `chain_state.json` + chain spec YAML via `provider.read_remote_file`, runs `chain.format_chain_status` on them, and emits a payload structurally identical to the local `chain status` output for the same inputs.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "No cloud module imports `chain_state.json` parsing logic that isn't `chain.load_chain_state` / `chain.format_chain_status`; a grep-based test asserts `megaplan/cloud/**/*.py` never references `chain_state` directly.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "run_shell"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`toolchains:` block in `cloud.yaml` accepts `rust`/`go`/`java` aliases and custom `{name, install}` entries; rendered `Dockerfile` contains the corresponding `RUN` stages and sets any required `ENV PATH` additions.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "With `toolchains:` omitted, the rendered `Dockerfile` is byte-identical to the v0.19.0 baseline (no breaking change to existing deployments).",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Redaction helper replaces known secret values (via spec.secrets + env), `NAME=value`/`NAME: value` patterns, and `sk-.../ghp_...` tokens with `***REDACTED***`; table-driven tests cover each case.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Every provider that depends on an external CLI (`railway`, `docker`, `ssh`) raises `CliError(code=\"provider_unavailable\")` with both what's missing and an install URL when the CLI is absent.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "All previously-passing tests in `tests/` still pass.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Public surface of `cloud.yaml` from v0.19.0 is preserved \u2014 every v0.19.0 spec still loads with no change in parsed `CloudSpec` fields.",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`cloud.yaml` docs (`cloud.yaml.tmpl`) and `README` Cloud runs section mention the `local` and `ssh` providers and the `toolchains:` block.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`docker compose` smoke test (`@pytest.mark.slow`) brings up the local-provider container, execs `which megaplan`, and tears it down cleanly \u2014 full cycle under 5 minutes.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests",
        "run_shell"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`LocalProvider` / `SshProvider` implementations each stay under ~200 LOC; if one balloons past that, split helpers or reuse the base.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Fly provider surfaces a distinct, helpful error (not `Unknown cloud provider`) explaining it's deferred, so users don't silently hit a wall.",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Reigh parity verified manually: `megaplan cloud bootstrap` against a tiny idea file completes successfully with the reigh env, using an in-tree cloud.yaml that replaces `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`.",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/` is deleted and replaced with a `MIGRATED.md` pointer; verification is out-of-repo so treated as a human-confirmed step.",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "`megaplan chain start --spec PATH` is a named subcommand under the existing `chain` parser and drives through the same `run_chain(...)` path the bare `megaplan chain --spec PATH` uses today; the bare form stays as a backcompat alias.",
    "`format_chain_status(spec, state)` lives in `megaplan.chain` (core) and is reused verbatim by `cloud status --chain` \u2014 this is what the brief means by 'do NOT reinvent the parser.'",
    "`--auto-start` on `megaplan init` runs `megaplan.auto.drive()` in-process and blocks until a terminal outcome; the init response payload embeds the driver outcome as `auto_outcome`.",
    "`toolchains:` accepts a mixed list: string aliases for built-in recipes (`rust`, `go`, `java`) or mappings `{name, install}` for custom install snippets. Empty list (default) preserves the v0.19.0 Python+Node image byte-for-byte.",
    "Local provider uses single-container `docker compose` with a bind-mounted `./workspace` host dir for the repo and a `.env` file for secrets, mirroring the Railway volume semantics as closely as possible.",
    "SSH provider uses plain `ssh`+`docker` over a bash login shell \u2014 rsync for deploy-dir upload with a `scp -r` fallback; no orchestration beyond a single named container.",
    "Fly provider is explicitly skipped per the 'OPTIONAL' note; `provider: fly` still errors with a dedicated 'future provider' message so users get a clear signal.",
    "Secret redaction is applied in the capture-then-emit paths (`_relay_output`, `logs --no-follow`, `status` response). Follow-mode `logs -f` is not intercepted; this limitation is documented in the redactor's docstring.",
    "Reigh retirement is documented as a runbook (new `docs/cloud-migration-from-reigh.md`); the actual `rm -rf` and `MIGRATED.md` write happens outside this repo and is a human-executed step.",
    "No version bump or CHANGELOG entry lands in this plan \u2014 sprint 2 ships as v0.20.0 but the bump + changelog commit is an explicit separate follow-up, per the brief's non-goals."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []
}

        Gate signals:
        {
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "Sprint 2 of the megaplan cloud feature \u2014 extend core megaplan ergonomics, add local + ssh providers, ship toolchain extensibility in cloud.yaml, retire the old reigh-megaplan-dev folder.\n\n### Context\nSprint 1 shipped in v0.19.0 on 2026-04-21: built-in megaplan cloud subcommand (init/build/deploy/status/attach/logs/exec/resume/down/destroy) with a Railway provider, extracted from the ad-hoc setup at ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/. Spec schema lives in megaplan/cloud/spec.py, templates in megaplan/cloud/templates/, providers in megaplan/cloud/providers/.\n\nArchitectural principle established in sprint 1 and now codified: CORE megaplan owns capabilities, CLOUD subcommands are thin transport wrappers. Do not reinvent chain state parsing in cloud. Do not grow a parallel 'cloud chain *' surface. Core gets the command; cloud wraps it for remote execution.\n\n### Sprint 2 deliverables\n\nCore megaplan additions (must work locally without any cloud dependency):\n1. megaplan chain start --spec <path> \u2014 validate spec, resolve referenced idea files, kick auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the 'init + auto' pair for chain runs.\n2. megaplan chain status --spec <path> \u2014 read chain_state.json, pretty-print current_milestone, completed, remaining, per-milestone status. Unit tests against synthetic chain states.\n3. megaplan init --idea-file <path> with optional --auto-start flag \u2014 read idea from a file instead of the positional CLI arg.\n\nCloud additions (thin wrappers over the new core commands):\n4. megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml> \u2014 upload spec, then cloud exec to call megaplan chain start remotely.\n5. megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file> \u2014 upload idea file, then cloud exec to call megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start remotely.\n6. megaplan cloud status --chain \u2014 fetch remote chain_state.json, invoke the core chain-status formatter on it. Do NOT reinvent the parser.\n\nNew providers:\n7. Local provider (docker compose) \u2014 fastest iteration target, no Railway/cloud dependency. Becomes the CI-friendly smoke-test target.\n8. SSH provider (plain docker-over-ssh) \u2014 'any host with docker + ssh access' story.\n\nToolchain extensibility:\n9. toolchains: block in cloud.yaml \u2014 declares one or more toolchains (rust, go, java, or custom with user-provided install snippet). Template renderer appends corresponding Dockerfile stages. Without this, the image is Python/Node only.\n\nHardening + retirement:\n10. Secret redaction in cloud logs output (match known secret name patterns, replace with ***REDACTED***).\n11. Clear error messages + documentation link when railway or docker CLI is missing at runtime.\n12. ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ migration \u2014 deploy cloud with a cloud.yaml matching reigh's current env, run a tiny plan to verify parity, then delete the folder and replace with a MIGRATED.md pointer.\n\n### Hard constraints\n- Core commands MUST work locally without any cloud code loaded.\n- Cloud wrappers MUST call out to core commands, not reimplement.\n- Fly provider is OPTIONAL (skip if time-pressured).\n- Preserve v0.19.0's public surface; this is additive.\n- No breaking cloud.yaml changes from v0.19.0 (add fields, don't rename).\n- Unit tests for every new core command; mocked-subprocess tests for every new provider; smoke test for local provider docker-compose lifecycle.\n\n### Explicit non-goals\n- No Kubernetes provider.\n- No chain-of-chains.\n- No cloud-specific chain status parser (the whole point of the core-first architecture).\n- No version bump in this plan \u2014 sprint 2 ships as v0.20.0 but the bump + CHANGELOG commit is a separate follow-up.\n\n### References\n- Sprint 1 landed in v0.19.0 (2026-04-21). Full CHANGELOG entry in CHANGELOG.md.\n- Reigh parity reference: ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ and megaplan/cloud/_reference/ (gitignored local copy of same).\n- Verified launcher: ./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan (system python is 3.8, fails on NotRequired).\n- Sprint 1 target retrospective: cloud subcommand CLI is already wired via megaplan/cloud/cli.py and dispatched from megaplan/cli.py.\n\nRobustness: standard \u2014 feature-rich with architecture decisions to enforce, multiple independent pieces worth independent critique, gate useful for scope discipline.",
    "significant_flags": 12,
    "unresolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "Cloud lifecycle: the proposed Local provider assumes a persistent compose project directory, but `run_cloud_cli()` currently materializes deploy assets in a per-command temporary directory and deletes them after each action.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",
        "concern": "Runner inputs: `cloud chain` only uploads the chain spec in the plan, but the core chain runner validates referenced milestone idea files and the example spec stores those as separate `/workspace/ideas/*.txt` inputs.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-003",
        "concern": "Core/cloud isolation: the plan's new isolation test cannot pass unless parser registration stops importing cloud modules for non-cloud commands.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-004",
        "concern": "Secrets: limiting redaction to non-follow paths leaves the default `megaplan cloud logs` workflow unredacted.",
        "category": "security",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "issue_hints-1",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: CLI surface: The brief's deliverable is `megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file>` as a thin wrapper around `megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start`, but the plan makes `--plan-name` mandatory. That is a new required argument not present in the requested surface, and the current core init path already auto-generates a name when `--name` is omitted (`megaplan/handlers.py:768-777`).",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "issue_hints-2",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: Secret redaction: The plan explicitly limits redaction to capture-then-emit paths and leaves `logs -f` unredacted, yet `megaplan cloud logs` currently defaults to follow mode (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:132-133`). Because the deliverable says 'Secret redaction in cloud logs output' without carving out the default path, the plan still leaves the main logs workflow exposed.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "correctness-1",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Init path: The proposed `--idea-file` change does not account for the current default naming and persisted state fields using `args.idea` directly (`megaplan/handlers.py:769,777`). If `idea` becomes optional and only `--idea-file` is passed, `slugify(args.idea)` and `state['idea'] = args.idea` will be wrong unless the plan also updates those lines to use the resolved idea text.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "correctness-2",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Chain bootstrap: The proposed `cloud chain` wrapper uploads only the top-level spec, but `megaplan.chain.validate_paths()` rejects any missing milestone idea file (`megaplan/chain.py:239-247`), and the bundled example spec points at separate `/workspace/ideas/*.txt` files (`megaplan/cloud/templates/chain.yaml.example:11-23`). As written, a remote `megaplan chain start --spec ...` would fail immediately unless the wrapper also stages the referenced idea files or rewrites the spec paths.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "scope",
        "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Core/cloud isolation: The plan treats the 'core commands work without cloud code loaded' requirement mainly as a new test, but the broader problem is in the parser bootstrap itself. `build_parser()` unconditionally imports `megaplan.cloud.cli` (`megaplan/cli.py:1003-1007`), so `main(argv=...)` cannot satisfy the proposed isolation test until parser registration is also made lazy or optional.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "all_locations-1",
        "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Local provider lifecycle: The current cloud CLI materializes deploy assets in a fresh `TemporaryDirectory` for each action and deletes it on exit (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:113-119,205-214`). A compose-based `local` provider that writes `.env`, bind-mounts `./workspace`, and later needs the same `docker-compose.yaml` for `exec`, `logs`, `down`, and `destroy` cannot work reliably from ephemeral directories. The plan needs persistent local deployment state, not just new provider classes and templates.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "all_locations-2",
        "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Provider glue: The current attach/session plumbing is Railway-specific at both the parser and dispatcher levels (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:57-62,182-188`). Adding `local` and `ssh` providers needs follow-through on that integration surface too; otherwise the CLI still exposes a Railway-only `--session` override and provider selection path that does not map cleanly onto the new backends.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "callers",
        "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Launcher path callers: both `megaplan.auto._run_megaplan()` and `megaplan.chain._init_plan()` shell out to a bare `megaplan` executable (`megaplan/auto.py:77-103`, `megaplan/chain.py:321-329`). The brief explicitly calls out `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan` as the verified launcher because the system Python is too old, so extending these flows with `init --auto-start` and more cloud wrappers broadens an existing caller-path ambiguity unless the plan also standardizes how subprocess invocations locate the correct interpreter.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      }
    ],

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "robust-review-v2"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-046",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-050",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",

      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a new critical issue is introduced on mobile. step 4.9 says to remove entrypage's mobile sticky bottom nav and 'use the same `footercontent` slot in submissioncarouselcard', but `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` renders `footercontent` inside `classname=\"hidden md:block\"`. in the current repo, the mobile sticky nav at `src/pages/entrypage.tsx:283-340` is the only mobile surface for back/next and the score toggle, so the revised plan would remove working mobile controls and replace them with a slot that is explicitly hidden on phones.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: if the implementation really wants `footercontent` to carry mobile actions, the plan must also include a supporting change in `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx` or render a separate mobile control surface outside the card. right now step 4.9 removes the mobile nav but does not list `submissioncarouselcard.tsx:217-221` as a place that must change, so the required glue for mobile controls is missing.",

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the prior missing locations in `megaplan/workers.py` and `tests/test_workers.py` are now covered, but one supporting-infrastructure path is still absent from the steps: the repo-root generated schema copy under `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` and the tests that read it directly. without either explicitly regenerating that file or changing those tests to materialize schemas in a temp root, the plan can still leave the runtime-schema copy out of sync with the raw registry.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-035",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the plan adds _set_active_step and _clear_active_step helpers to handlers.py. _run_worker (handlers.py:135) is modified to accept a `resolved` parameter. but _run_worker is also called directly by handle_critique's sequential fallback at line 801 and 803. these calls currently don't pass `resolved`. the plan's step 2.6 for handle_critique says to set active_step after resolution at line 794 \u2014 but the _run_worker calls at 801/803 would re-resolve the agent internally (since `resolved` isn't passed). this means the agent is resolved twice on the critique sequential path. the double-resolution is wasteful but correct (same result). however, to match the plan's goal of 'resolve_agent_mode() is called once per handler invocation', the resolved tuple should be passed to these _run_worker calls too.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",

      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
Sprint 2 of the megaplan cloud feature — extend core megaplan ergonomics, add local + ssh providers, ship toolchain extensibility in cloud.yaml, retire the old reigh-megaplan-dev folder.

### Context
Sprint 1 shipped in v0.19.0 on 2026-04-21: built-in megaplan cloud subcommand (init/build/deploy/status/attach/logs/exec/resume/down/destroy) with a Railway provider, extracted from the ad-hoc setup at ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/. Spec schema lives in megaplan/cloud/spec.py, templates in megaplan/cloud/templates/, providers in megaplan/cloud/providers/.

Architectural principle established in sprint 1 and now codified: CORE megaplan owns capabilities, CLOUD subcommands are thin transport wrappers. Do not reinvent chain state parsing in cloud. Do not grow a parallel 'cloud chain *' surface. Core gets the command; cloud wraps it for remote execution.

### Sprint 2 deliverables

Core megaplan additions (must work locally without any cloud dependency):
1. megaplan chain start --spec <path> — validate spec, resolve referenced idea files, kick auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the 'init + auto' pair for chain runs.
2. megaplan chain status --spec <path> — read chain_state.json, pretty-print current_milestone, completed, remaining, per-milestone status. Unit tests against synthetic chain states.
3. megaplan init --idea-file <path> with optional --auto-start flag — read idea from a file instead of the positional CLI arg.

Cloud additions (thin wrappers over the new core commands):
4. megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml> — upload spec, then cloud exec to call megaplan chain start remotely.
5. megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file> — upload idea file, then cloud exec to call megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start remotely.
6. megaplan cloud status --chain — fetch remote chain_state.json, invoke the core chain-status formatter on it. Do NOT reinvent the parser.

New providers:
7. Local provider (docker compose) — fastest iteration target, no Railway/cloud dependency. Becomes the CI-friendly smoke-test target.
8. SSH provider (plain docker-over-ssh) — 'any host with docker + ssh access' story.

Toolchain extensibility:
9. toolchains: block in cloud.yaml — declares one or more toolchains (rust, go, java, or custom with user-provided install snippet). Template renderer appends corresponding Dockerfile stages. Without this, the image is Python/Node only.

Hardening + retirement:
10. Secret redaction in cloud logs output (match known secret name patterns, replace with ***REDACTED***).
11. Clear error messages + documentation link when railway or docker CLI is missing at runtime.
12. ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ migration — deploy cloud with a cloud.yaml matching reigh's current env, run a tiny plan to verify parity, then delete the folder and replace with a MIGRATED.md pointer.

### Hard constraints
- Core commands MUST work locally without any cloud code loaded.
- Cloud wrappers MUST call out to core commands, not reimplement.
- Fly provider is OPTIONAL (skip if time-pressured).
- Preserve v0.19.0's public surface; this is additive.
- No breaking cloud.yaml changes from v0.19.0 (add fields, don't rename).
- Unit tests for every new core command; mocked-subprocess tests for every new provider; smoke test for local provider docker-compose lifecycle.

### Explicit non-goals
- No Kubernetes provider.
- No chain-of-chains.
- No cloud-specific chain status parser (the whole point of the core-first architecture).
- No version bump in this plan — sprint 2 ships as v0.20.0 but the bump + CHANGELOG commit is a separate follow-up.

### References
- Sprint 1 landed in v0.19.0 (2026-04-21). Full CHANGELOG entry in CHANGELOG.md.
- Reigh parity reference: ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ and megaplan/cloud/_reference/ (gitignored local copy of same).
- Verified launcher: ./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan (system python is 3.8, fails on NotRequired).
- Sprint 1 target retrospective: cloud subcommand CLI is already wired via megaplan/cloud/cli.py and dispatched from megaplan/cli.py.

Robustness: standard — feature-rich with architecture decisions to enforce, multiple independent pieces worth independent critique, gate useful for scope discipline.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Cloud Sprint 2 — Core Ergonomics, Local/SSH Providers, Toolchain Block, Reigh Retirement (Rev 2)

## Overview

**Goal.** Three new **core** commands (`chain start`, `chain status` pretty-print, `init --idea-file`/`--auto-start`) with zero cloud dependency, three **cloud** wrappers that thin-call them (`cloud chain`, `cloud bootstrap`, `cloud status --chain`), two new **providers** (`local` docker-compose, `ssh` docker-over-ssh), a **toolchains** extensibility block in `cloud.yaml`, full log **secret redaction** (including the default `logs -f` path), clear missing-CLI errors, and retirement of `~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/`.

**Architectural contract (from sprint 1).** Core owns capability; cloud wraps transport. No re-parsing `chain_state.json` in cloud code.

**What Rev 2 adds over the first plan.** Rev 1 missed five plumbing gaps the gate caught. Rev 2 scopes them in explicitly:
- **Parser isolation is a code change, not just a test** — `build_parser` currently imports `megaplan.cloud.cli` unconditionally (`megaplan/cli.py:1006-1007`); lazy registration is now Step 4.
- **Local provider needs persistent state** — replace the per-command `TemporaryDirectory` pattern (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:113-119, 205-214`) with a persistent per-project directory (`~/.megaplan/cloud/<proj>/`). Step 7.
- **`cloud chain` must stage every file the core runner opens** — not just the top-level spec, but every `milestones[*].idea` path. Step 15.
- **Follow-mode log redaction** — `logs -f` is the default (`megaplan/cloud/cli.py:132-133`); streaming redactor is now Step 17, not a carved-out gap.
- **Init handler resolves `idea_text` before using it** — `slugify(args.idea)` / `state["idea"] = args.idea` break when only `--idea-file` is set. Fixed in Step 3.
- **`--plan-name` on `cloud bootstrap` is optional** — core already auto-generates from `slugify(idea)`. Step 15.
- **Provider glue generalization** — `--session` is Railway-only; gate it on `provider == "railway"` and document. Step 8.
- **Launcher path standardization** — `auto._run_megaplan` and `chain._init_plan` shell out to bare `megaplan`; switch to `sys.executable -m megaplan` since the brief names that as the verified launcher. Step 5.

**Current shape.**
- Core: `megaplan/chain.py` has `ChainSpec`/`ChainState`/`load_chain_state`/`validate_paths`, drives via `auto.drive()`, exposes `chain status --spec` as a JSON dumper. `megaplan/handlers.py:handle_init` takes `idea` as a positional arg only.
- Cloud: `megaplan/cloud/{cli.py,spec.py,template.py,providers/base.py,providers/railway.py}`. `spec.py:167-172` rejects non-railway providers. `providers/base.py:52-54` has a hardcoded `if name != "railway"` guard. `cli.py` uses `TemporaryDirectory` for deploy dirs.

**Constraints that matter.**
- No core module imports anything from `megaplan.cloud` — verified by both a test and the lazy-parser change.
- `cloud.yaml` v0.19.0 spec keeps loading unchanged; all additions are additive.
- Fly provider skipped (brief: optional).
- No version bump / CHANGELOG in this plan — separate follow-up commit.

## Phase 1: Core-first commands + parser & launcher hygiene

### Step 1: Add `megaplan chain start` subcommand (`megaplan/chain.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Extend** `build_chain_parser` (`megaplan/chain.py:528`) with a `start` subparser under `chain_sub`. Flags: `--spec <path>` required, `--no-git-refresh`. Keep the bare `megaplan chain --spec` path working so existing shell wrappers don't break.
2. **Wire** `run_chain_cli` (`megaplan/chain.py:558`) to dispatch `chain start` to the same `run_chain(...)` driver the bare command uses. `chain start` already gets `validate_paths` + `run_chain` via the existing code path.
3. **Update** `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-chain` to call `megaplan chain start --spec "$1"` so the deployed container boots via the named entry.

### Step 2: Add `chain status` pretty-print formatter (`megaplan/chain.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `format_chain_status(spec: ChainSpec, state: ChainState) -> dict` in `megaplan/chain.py` returning:
   - `current_milestone` — `{label, index}` or `None` (pre-seed)
   - `completed` — list of `{label, plan, status}` copies from `state.completed`
   - `remaining` — list of `{label, idea}` for milestones past `current_milestone_index`
   - `per_milestone` — one entry per milestone: `{label, status}` ∈ `completed | in_progress | pending`
   - Pass-through: `seed_plan`, `current_plan_name`, `last_state`
2. **Rewrite** the `chain status` branch in `run_chain_cli`: emit the structured JSON to stdout (adding `summary` key = formatter output; preserve existing `success` / `spec` / `milestone_count` / `seed_plan` / `chain_state` keys — additive only) and a human-readable block to stderr via `writer`.
3. **Export** `format_chain_status` from `megaplan.chain` so `cloud status --chain` (Step 16) reuses it verbatim.

### Step 3: `megaplan init --idea-file` with optional `--auto-start` (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Adjust** `init_parser` (`megaplan/cli.py:835-852`): make `idea` positional optional (`nargs="?"`); add `--idea-file <path>`; add `--auto-start`.
2. **Update** `handle_init` (`megaplan/handlers.py:728`). **The key correctness fix (addresses correctness-1):**
   - At the top of the handler, compute `idea_text: str`:
     - If `args.idea` and `args.idea_file` both set → `CliError("invalid_args", "--idea-file is mutually exclusive with the positional idea argument")`.
     - If `--idea-file`: read UTF-8, strip, reject empty with `CliError("invalid_args", "idea file is empty")`.
     - If positional: use `args.idea`.
     - If neither: `CliError("invalid_args", "either positional idea or --idea-file is required")`.
   - Replace **every** downstream reference to `args.idea` with `idea_text`. Specifically:
     - `handlers.py:769` — `slugify(args.idea)` → `slugify(idea_text)`
     - `handlers.py:777` — `"idea": args.idea` → `"idea": idea_text`
   - When `--auto-start` is set, after `save_state(...)` and the init response is built, call `megaplan.auto.drive(plan_name, cwd=root, ...)` **in-process** with default driver knobs. Embed `DriverOutcome` fields under an `auto_outcome` key in the response. `idea_text` stays referenced via closure — no lingering `args.idea` reads.
3. **Import rule unchanged:** `handlers.py` → `auto` (already transitive); no cloud import introduced.

### Step 4: Lazy cloud subparser registration (`megaplan/cli.py`) — resolves FLAG-003, scope
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** the unconditional `from megaplan.cloud.cli import build_cloud_parser` + call at `megaplan/cli.py:1006-1007` with a lazy stub that registers a `cloud` subparser whose action is "delegate to `megaplan.cloud.cli` at run time":
   - In `build_parser`, add a single-line `cloud_stub = subparsers.add_parser("cloud", help="Provider-backed megaplan cloud runners (loaded on demand)", add_help=False)`. The stub declares no subcommands; full parsing is deferred.
   - In `main`, when `args.command == "cloud"` (or when `argv[0] == "cloud"` pre-parse), **then** import `megaplan.cloud.cli`, call `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` on a freshly-built inner parser, reparse `argv[1:]`, and dispatch `run_cloud_cli(root, args)`.
   - `megaplan cloud --help` remains discoverable: when the stub detects `--help` / no subcommand, it imports cloud.cli on demand and defers to the real parser's help output. Matches gate warning about preserving discoverability.
2. **Guardrail test** (`tests/test_core_without_cloud.py`): monkey-patch `sys.modules["megaplan.cloud"] = None` so any `import megaplan.cloud*` raises, then run `main(["chain", "status", "--spec", str(p)])` and `main(["init", "--project-dir", ..., "--idea-file", ..., "--auto-start"])` via `subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "megaplan", ...])` with `PYTHONPATH` overrides OR via a fresh interpreter with `megaplan.cloud` stubbed out. Both succeed without triggering the ImportError sentinel.

### Step 5: Standardize subprocess launcher (`megaplan/auto.py`, `megaplan/chain.py`) — resolves callers
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** `["megaplan", *args]` at `megaplan/auto.py:97` with `[sys.executable, "-m", "megaplan", *args]`. The brief's verified launcher is `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan`; `sys.executable` resolves to whichever interpreter the parent is running under, matching that.
2. **Replace** `["megaplan", "init", ...]`, `["megaplan", "status", ...]` at `megaplan/chain.py:323, 271` with the same `sys.executable -m megaplan` prefix.
3. **Keep** bare `megaplan` in shell wrappers under `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/` — those run inside the container where `megaplan` is installed on PATH as an entrypoint. This change is local-shell-only.
4. **Test update:** `tests/test_auto.py` / `tests/test_chain.py` assertions that assert exact argv need to be updated to match the new prefix. Grep the tests first; narrow surgical edit.

### Step 6: Core tests (`tests/test_chain.py`, `tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py`, `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** `tests/test_chain.py`:
   - `test_chain_start_invokes_driver` — mocks `auto.drive`, asserts `run_chain_cli(... chain_action="start", spec=...)` hits the same validator + driver path.
   - `test_format_chain_status_pretty` — synthetic `ChainSpec` (seed + 3 milestones) + `ChainState` (seed done, 1 completed, 1 in-progress, 1 remaining); asserts all five formatter fields.
2. **Add** `tests/test_handle_init_idea_file.py`:
   - `test_init_reads_idea_file`
   - `test_init_rejects_both_positional_and_idea_file`
   - `test_init_rejects_empty_idea_file`
   - `test_init_slugify_uses_resolved_idea_text` (regression guard for correctness-1 — asserts `plan_name` contains a slug derived from the file contents when only `--idea-file` is passed)
   - `test_init_state_idea_is_file_contents`
   - `test_init_auto_start_invokes_drive` (mocked `auto.drive`, asserts called once with plan name + root).
3. **Add** `tests/test_core_without_cloud.py` per Step 4: runs `chain status` + `init --idea-file --auto-start` with `megaplan.cloud` unimportable.
